#### 2026-07-19T13:21:20.975Z  ·  `trig_timer_brief`
*tags: task_kickoff, actor_response*
> Delivered the initial brief for Sprint 2 to the learner, outlining requirements, deliverables, deadlines, and support routing.
- **manager → learner:** Learner B6,
  
  We are commencing Sprint 2. You are expected to translate your Sprint 1 research into a clear product strategy, a prioritized backlog, and a scoped MVP that reflects real product tradeoffs.
  
  **Task Context**
  In this sprint, you will align Sprints.ai’s product vision with structured execution. This requires you to establish clear success metrics, map our current technical stack, prioritize solution bets, and design a validated learning roadmap.
  
  **What You Must Build or Analyze**
  1. Opportunity Solution Tree: Map a specific, measurable outcome to opportunities identified in Sprint 1 and their corresponding solution bets. Every node must be traceable to your previous research.
  2. North Star Metric + Metric Tree: Define a single leading-indicator North Star Metric. Connect it to 3–4 input metrics, 1–2 lagging outcomes, and at least 1 guardrail metric, noting current tracking gaps.
  3. RICE-Scored Backlog: Score your top 5 solution bets using Reach, Impact, Confidence, and Effort. Provide explicit component reasoning and a 2-sentence tradeoff rationale for each bet.
  4. MVP Scope Doc: Use the MoSCoW method to categorize features. Limit your "Must Haves" to a maximum of 3 items, and write strategic descoping rationales for all other items.
  5. 4-Week Product Roadmap: Create a validation-focused roadmap where each weekly milestone is tied to a learning goal and an observable success signal.
  6. Tech Stack Mapping: Document a high-level structured overview of Sprints.ai's delivery, data, analytics, and integrations, explicitly flagging any assumptions.
  7. Alignment Sync: Prepare a written summary of your MVP scope and roadmap to send to your mentor 24 hours prior to the formal sync on Day 5.
  
  **Required Deliverables**
  You must submit a single, comprehensive document containing:
  - Opportunity Solution Tree
  - North Star Metric + Metric Tree
  - RICE-Scored Backlog
  - MVP Scope Doc
  - 4-Week Product Roadmap
  - Tech Stack Mapping
  
  **Assets and Links**
  There are no external file attachments provided for this task. You must work with the existing parameters of Sprints.ai. If you identify missing platform analytics or documentation, logically outline your assumptions and proceed with a defensible strategy.
  
  **Deadline**
  The deadline for this submission is Thursday, July 23, 2026, at 11:59 PM Cairo time. The deadline stands. Scope or timeline adjustments will not be granted.
  
  **Success Standard**
  Your work must demonstrate strategic rigor. I expect to see honest, evidence-backed RICE scores rather than arbitrary high-impact ratings, a strictly scoped MVP with no more than 3 Must-Haves, and a roadmap focused on learning rather than mere feature delivery.
  
  **Support Routing**
  Technical questions and draft feedback go to your mentor. For administrative or program coordination issues, contact Nadeen Diaa.

#### 2026-07-26T08:52:57.726Z  ·  `trig_timer_brief`
*tags: task_kickoff, actor_response*
> Delivered Product Sprint 3 kickoff brief to learner with detailed deliverable specs, success standards, and routing guidelines.
- **manager → learner:** Learner B6, here is your task brief for Product Sprint 3. You are expected to move from concept to a fully clickable interactive prototype and complete supporting product documentation.
  
  TASK CONTEXT:
  This sprint focuses on turning your AI feature concept from Sprint 1 into a functional, clickable UI and engineering-ready specifications. Scope ruthlessly and account for system failure states and edge cases rather than solely designing for happy paths.
  
  WHAT YOU MUST BUILD:
  1. An interactive, hi-fi prototype of your AI capability using tools like Lovable or v0, covering the end-to-end user flow.
  2. A single-page Product Requirement Document (PRD).
  3. 5 to 8 user stories with Given/When/Then acceptance criteria.
  4. A live Sprint Board with a clear sprint goal.
  5. A 1-page Technical Integration Note showing how the capability connects to the platform stack.
  
  REQUIRED DELIVERABLES:
  - Interactive AI Prototype: Hi-fi, production-quality screens (not wireframes) built using an AI prototyping tool (e.g., Lovable or v0). Must feature a consistent visual design system, cover the full core interaction flow, include real UI states (loading, empty, error, success), and be accessible via a public, shareable link that anyone can navigate unaided.
  - 1-Page PRD: A lean 1-page document containing all 7 required sections: Problem, Primary User, Goals, In Scope, Out of Scope, Constraints (technical, business, and AI/model constraints covering cost, latency, accuracy, and data availability), and at least 3 Open Questions.
  - User Stories & Acceptance Criteria: 5 to 8 stories in "As a / I want to / So that" format based on your Sprint 1 persona. Each story must have at least 2 acceptance criteria in Given/When/Then format. At least one story per flow must explicitly cover edge cases (e.g., API timeout, missing context/data, mobile access).
  - Sprint Board Setup: A configured PM tool board containing epics, stories, tasks, and an explicit sprint goal, accompanied by a board screenshot and brief walkthrough.
  - Technical Integration Note: A 1-page architectural sketch mapping the feature to Sprints.ai's frontend, backend, data layer, and AI/model layer (prompting, API, or fine-tuning strategy).
  
  ASSETS AND LINKS:
  No external file assets are provided. You are expected to leverage AI prototyping tools such as Lovable or v0 to build the UI deliverables.
  
  DEADLINE:
  Thursday, July 30, 2026, at 11:59:59 PM Cairo time. Deadlines are strictly enforced; I do not grant extensions on request.
  
  SUCCESS STANDARD:
  Your work must be complete, production-ready, and fully navigable. The PRD and user stories must be precise enough that an engineering team could build the feature without asking clarifying questions, and all required failure states must be documented.
  
  SUPPORT ROUTING AND SUBMISSION:
  - Final Submission: Submit your final deliverables directly to your mentor.
  - Technical Guidance & Draft Reviews: Route all implementation questions, technical guidance, and draft feedback requests to your mentor.
  - Technical Blockers & Access Issues: Post in the team/internship group for assistance from internship admins.
  - Program & Administrative Matters: Route administrative or personal program questions to Nadeen Diaa, Internship Coordinator.
  - Scope & Schedule Authority: All scope adjustments, deadline reviews, and priority decisions remain under senior management authority.
  
  Review these expectations and begin execution immediately.

  عشان الشغل يستوفي المعايير بالكامل، في نقطتين محتاجين نركز عليهم:
  - الـ Sprint Board ناقصه screenshot واضح جوه الفايل يبيّن الـ board من الـ PM tool بالـ epics والـ sprint goal.
  - الـ PRD محتاج يبرز سكشنز الـ Problem والـ Primary User والـ Goals بشكل واضح، وتكملة الـ AI constraints بتوضيح نقط الـ cost والـ data availability.
  
  ضيف الـ screenshot وحسّن السكشنز دي في الـ PRD، وابعثلي الفايل بعد التعديل عشان نراجع النسخة الجديدة.

failures?\\nWhich review categories are suitable for AI-assisted evaluation and which require fully manual review?\\nWhat success threshold will determine whether the MVP should move into full implementation?\\n=========================================================================\\nUser Stories & Acceptance Criteria\\nStory 1: AI Rubric Evaluation\\nAs a mentor, I want AI to evaluate submissions against rubric criteria so that I spend less time performing repetitive assessments.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven a valid submission and an associated rubric\\nWhen the mentor requests AI analysis\\nThen the system generates scores for each rubric criterion.\\nGiven rubric scores have been generated\\nWhen the mentor views the analysis\\nThen an explanation is displayed for each score.\\nStory 2: AI Draft Feedback Generation\\nAs a mentor, I want AI-generated draft feedback so that I can review submissions faster.\\nAcceptance Criteria\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_5\", \"content\": \"Acceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI analysis has completed successfully\\nWhen draft feedback is generated\\nThen the feedback is displayed in an editable format.\\nGiven AI-generated feedback is displayed\\nWhen the mentor edits the feedback\\nThen the updated version is saved before publishing.\\nStory 3: Mentor Approval Workflow\\nAs a mentor, I want to approve or reject AI-generated recommendations so that feedback quality remains under human control.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI recommendations are available\\nWhen the mentor selects Approve\\nThen the feedback is marked as approved and ready for publication.\\nGiven AI recommendations are available\\nWhen the mentor selects Reject\\nThen the AI recommendations are discarded and manual review remains available.\\nStory 4: Intern Feedback Delivery\\nAs an intern, I want to receive reviewed feedback quickly so that I can continue progressing through my internship tasks.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven mentor approval has been completed\\nWhen feedback is published\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_6\", \"content\": \"Acceptance Criteria\\nGiven mentor approval has been completed\\nWhen feedback is published\\nThen the intern receives access to the feedback.\\nGiven feedback is available\\nWhen the intern opens the review page\\nThen rubric scores and mentor feedback are displayed.\\nStory 5: AI Confidence Transparency\\nAs a mentor, I want confidence indicators for AI-generated reviews so that I can determine the appropriate level of oversight.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI analysis has completed\\nWhen confidence is calculated\\nThen a High, Medium, or Low confidence label is displayed.\\nGiven the confidence score is Low\\nWhen the mentor views the results\\nThen a warning message is shown recommending additional review.\\nStory 6: AI Timeout Handling (Edge Case)\\nAs a mentor, I want to continue reviewing submissions when AI services fail so that my work is never blocked.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven an AI request exceeds the allowed response time\\nWhen the request times out\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_7\", \"content\": \"Given an AI request exceeds the allowed response time\\nWhen the request times out\\nThen the system displays a timeout message and retry option.\\nGiven an AI timeout occurs\\nWhen the mentor selects Continue Manually\\nThen the manual review workflow becomes available immediately.\\nStory 7: Missing Rubric Handling (Edge Case)\\nAs a mentor, I want the system to handle missing rubric data gracefully so that reviews can still be completed.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven a submission has no associated rubric\\nWhen AI analysis is requested\\nThen the system displays a missing rubric warning.\\nGiven the missing rubric warning is displayed\\nWhen the mentor chooses to proceed\\nThen manual review remains available without AI assistance.\\n=========================================================================\\nSprint Out of Scope\\nThe MVP will not include:\\nFully autonomous grading or scoring.\\nAutomatic publication of AI feedback without mentor approval.\\nPersonalized learning plans.\\nAI mentor chatbot functionality.\\nVoice-based interactions.\\nPeer review workflows.\\nAutomated intern performance ranking.\\nThese capabilities may be considered in future iterations after MVP validation.\\n6. Constraints\\nTechnical Constraints\\nRubric quality and structure may vary between projects.\\nHistorical review data may be incomplete or inconsistent.\\nExisting platform integrations must not disrupt the current review workflow\\nBusiness Constraints\\nHuman review and approval must remain mandatory.\\nReview quality cannot decline while improving speed.\\nSolution must support future scaling across multiple internship tracks.\\nAI / Model Constraints\\nLatency\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_4\", \"content\": \"What fallback workflow should be triggered after repeated AI service failures?\\nWhich review categories are suitable for AI-assisted evaluation and which require fully manual review?\\nWhat success threshold will determine whether the MVP should move into full implementation?\\n=========================================================================\\nUser Stories & Acceptance Criteria\\nStory 1: AI Rubric Evaluation\\nAs a mentor, I want AI to evaluate submissions against rubric criteria so that I spend less time performing repetitive assessments.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven a valid submission and an associated rubric\\nWhen the mentor requests AI analysis\\nThen the system generates scores for each rubric criterion.\\nGiven rubric scores have been generated\\nWhen the mentor views the analysis\\nThen an explanation is displayed for each score.\\nStory 2: AI Draft Feedback Generation\\nAs a mentor, I want AI-generated draft feedback so that I can review submissions faster.\\nAcceptance Criteria\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_5\", \"content\": \"Acceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI analysis has completed successfully\\nWhen draft feedback is generated\\nThen the feedback is displayed in an editable format.\\nGiven AI-generated feedback is displayed\\nWhen the mentor edits the feedback\\nThen the updated version is saved before publishing.\\nStory 3: Mentor Approval Workflow\\nAs a mentor, I want to approve or reject AI-generated recommendations so that feedback quality remains under human control.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI recommendations are available\\nWhen the mentor selects Approve\\nThen the feedback is marked as approved and ready for publication.\\nGiven AI recommendations are available\\nWhen the mentor selects Reject\\nThen the AI recommendations are discarded and manual review remains available.\\nStory 4: Intern Feedback Delivery\\nAs an intern, I want to receive reviewed feedback quickly so that I can continue progressing through my internship tasks.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven mentor approval has been completed\\nWhen feedback is published\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_6\", \"content\": \"Acceptance Criteria\\nGiven mentor approval has been completed\\nWhen feedback is published\\nThen the intern receives access to the feedback.\\nGiven feedback is available\\nWhen the intern opens the review page\\nThen rubric scores and mentor feedback are displayed.\\nStory 5: AI Confidence Transparency\\nAs a mentor, I want confidence indicators for AI-generated reviews so that I can determine the appropriate level of oversight.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI analysis has completed\\nWhen confidence is calculated\\nThen a High, Medium, or Low confidence label is displayed.\\nGiven the confidence score is Low\\nWhen the mentor views the results\\nThen a warning message is shown recommending additional review.\\nStory 6: AI Timeout Handling (Edge Case)\\nAs a mentor, I want to continue reviewing submissions when AI services fail so that my work is never blocked.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven an AI request exceeds the allowed response time\\nWhen the request times out\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_7\", \"content\": \"Given an AI request exceeds the allowed response time\\nWhen the request times out\\nThen the system displays a timeout message and retry option.\\nGiven an AI timeout occurs\\nWhen the mentor selects Continue Manually\\nThen the manual review workflow becomes available immediately.\\nStory 7: Missing Rubric Handling (Edge Case)\\nAs a mentor, I want the system to handle missing rubric data gracefully so that reviews can still be completed.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven a submission has no associated rubric\\nWhen AI analysis is requested\\nThen the system displays a missing rubric warning.\\nGiven the missing rubric warning is displayed\\nWhen the mentor chooses to proceed\\nThen manual review remains available without AI assistance.\\n=========================================================================\\nSprint sprint prioritizes the core mentor workflow and focuses on reducing repetitive review effort. Features outside the direct review bottleneck were intentionally excluded to maintain a focused MVP scope.\\nJira analysis\\nThen the system generates scores for each rubric criterion.\\nGiven rubric scores have been generated\\nWhen the mentor views the analysis\\nThen an explanation is displayed for each \"Acceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI analysis has completed successfully\\nWhen draft feedback is generated\\nThen the feedback is displayed in an editable format.\\nGiven AI-generated feedback is displayed\\nWhen the mentor edits the feedback\\nThen the updated version is saved before publishing.\\nStory 3: Mentor Approval Workflow\\nAs a mentor, I want to approve or reject AI-generated recommendations so that feedback quality remains under human control.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI recommendations are available\\nWhen the mentor selects Approve\\nThen the feedback is marked as approved and ready for publication.\\nGiven AI recommendations are available\\nWhen the mentor selects Reject\\nThen the AI recommendations are discarded and manual review remains available.\\nStory The opportunity is to reduce repetitive review effort through AI-assisted review workflows while maintaining human oversight and feedback quality.\\nPrimary User Persona\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_2\", \"content\": \"• Deliver faster feedback to interns.\\n• Increase consistency of review quality.\\nProduct Success Metrics:\\n• 30% reduction in TTFF.\\n• 20% reduction in mentor review time per submission.\\n• Mentor Override Rate below 40%.\\n• No reduction in feedback satisfaction scores.\\nAI Constraints\\nLatency:\\nAI review generation should complete within 10 seconds under normal operating conditions.\\nAccuracy:\\nAI-generated rubric assessments should achieve at least 80% agreement with mentor evaluations during testing.\\nCost Constraints:\\nThe solution must operate within approved AI infrastructure budgets. Average inference cost per review must remain sustainable at scale.\\nData Availability Constraints:\\nHistorical review data may be incomplete, inconsistent, or unavailable for certain submission categories. The MVP must function using structured rubrics even when historical examples are limited.\\nReliability Constraints:\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_3\", \"content\": \"Reliability Constraints:\\nThe system must provide fallback workflows when AI services fail, timeout, return low-confidence outputs, or encounter missing rubric data.\\n=========================================================================\\n7. Open Questions\\nWhat confidence score threshold should require mandatory mentor review?\\nWhich rubric formats are currently standardized across the platform?\\nHow should mentor edits be captured and used to improve future AI recommendations?\\nWhat level of explanation is necessary to build mentor trust in AI-generated feedback?\\nWhat fallback workflow should be triggered after repeated AI service failures?\\nWhich review categories are suitable for AI-assisted evaluation and which require fully manual review?\\nWhat success threshold will determine whether the MVP should move into full implementation?\\n=========================================================================\\nUser Stories & Acceptance Criteria\\nStory 1: AI Rubric Evaluation\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_4\", \"content\": \"User Stories & Acceptance Criteria\\nStory 1: AI Rubric Evaluation\\nAs a mentor, I want AI to evaluate submissions against rubric criteria so that I spend less time performing repetitive assessments.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven a valid submission and an associated rubric\\nWhen the mentor requests AI analysis\\nThen the system generates scores for each rubric criterion.\\nGiven rubric scores have been generated\\nWhen the mentor views the analysis\\nThen an explanation is displayed for each score.\\nStory 2: AI Draft Feedback Generation\\nAs a mentor, I want AI-generated draft feedback so that I can review submissions faster.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI analysis has completed successfully\\nWhen draft feedback is generated\\nThen the feedback is displayed in an editable format.\\nGiven AI-generated feedback is displayed\\nWhen the mentor edits the feedback\\nThen the updated version is saved before publishing.\\nStory 3: Mentor Approval Workflow\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_5\", \"content\": \"Then the updated version is saved before publishing.\\nStory 3: Mentor Approval Workflow\\nAs a mentor, I want to approve or reject AI-generated recommendations so that feedback quality remains under human control.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI recommendations are available\\nWhen the mentor selects Approve\\nThen the feedback is marked as approved and ready for publication.\\nGiven AI recommendations are available\\nWhen the mentor selects Reject\\nThen the AI recommendations are discarded and manual review remains available.\\nStory 4: Intern Feedback Delivery\\nAs an intern, I want to receive reviewed feedback quickly so that I can continue progressing through my internship tasks.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven mentor approval has been completed\\nWhen feedback is published\\nThen the intern receives access to the feedback.\\nGiven feedback is available\\nWhen the intern opens the review page\\nThen rubric scores and mentor feedback are displayed.\\nStory 5: AI Confidence Transparency\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_6\", \"content\": \"Then rubric scores and mentor feedback are displayed.\\nStory 5: AI Confidence Transparency\\nAs a mentor, I want confidence indicators for AI-generated reviews so that I can determine the appropriate level of oversight.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI analysis has completed\\nWhen confidence is calculated\\nThen a High, Medium, or Low confidence label is displayed.\\nGiven the confidence score is Low\\nWhen the mentor views the results\\nThen a warning message is shown recommending additional review.\\nStory 6: AI Timeout Handling (Edge Case)\\nAs a mentor, I want to continue reviewing submissions when AI services fail so that my work is never blocked.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven an AI request exceeds the allowed response time\\nWhen the request times out\\nThen the system displays a timeout message and retry option.\\nGiven an AI timeout occurs\\nWhen the mentor selects Continue Manually\\nThen the manual review workflow becomes available immediately.\\nStory 7: Missing Rubric Handling (Edge Case)\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_7\", \"content\": \"Story 7: Missing Rubric Handling (Edge Case)\\nAs a mentor, I want the system to handle missing rubric data gracefully so that reviews can still be completed.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven a submission has no associated rubric\\nWhen AI analysis is requested\\nThen the system displays a missing rubric warning.\\nGiven the missing rubric warning is displayed\\nWhen the mentor chooses to proceed\\nThen manual review remains available without AI assistance.\\n=========================================================================\\nSprint Board Setup & Walkthrough\\nSprint Goal\\nValidate whether AI-assisted first-pass reviews can reduce mentor review effort and improve Median Time-to-First-Feedback (TTFF) while maintaining feedback quality.\\nBoard Structure\\nEpic: AI Review Assistant MVP\\nThis epic contains all work required to validate the MVP hypothesis that AI-generated rubric assessments and draft feedback can reduce mentor workload.\\nUser Stories Included\\nAI Rubric Evaluation\\nAI Draft Feedback Generation\"}]}]}, {\"id\": 3, \"requirement\": \"User stories + acceptance criteria\", \"description\": \"5-8 persona-driven user stories in 'As a / I want to / So \"content\": \"Then rubric scores and mentor feedback are displayed.\\nStory 5: AI Confidence Transparency\\nAs a mentor, I want confidence indicators for AI-generated reviews so that I can determine the appropriate level of oversight.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI analysis has completed\\nWhen confidence is calculated\\nThen a High, Medium, or Low confidence label is displayed.\\nGiven the confidence score is Low\\nWhen the mentor views the results\\nThen a warning message is shown recommending additional review.\\nStory 6: AI Timeout Handling (Edge Case)\\nAs a mentor, I want to continue reviewing submissions when AI services fail so that my work is never blocked.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven an AI request exceeds the allowed response time\\nWhen the request times out\\nThen the system displays a timeout message and retry option.\\nGiven an AI timeout occurs\\nWhen the mentor selects Continue Manually\\nThen the manual review workflow becomes available immediately.\\nStory 7: Missing Rubric Handling (Edge Case)\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_7\", \"content\": \"Story 7: Missing Rubric Handling (Edge Case)\\nAs a mentor, I want the system to handle missing rubric data gracefully so that reviews can still be completed.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven a submission has no associated rubric\\nWhen AI analysis is requested\\nThen the system displays a missing rubric warning.\\nGiven the missing rubric warning is displayed\\nWhen the mentor chooses to proceed\\nThen manual review remains available without AI assistance.\\n=========================================================================\\nSprint Board Setup & Walkthrough\\nSprint Goal\\nValidate whether AI-assisted first-pass reviews can reduce mentor review effort and improve Median Time-to-First-Feedback (TTFF) while maintaining feedback quality.\\nBoard Structure\\nEpic: AI Review Assistant MVP\\nThis epic contains all work required to validate the MVP hypothesis that AI-generated rubric assessments and draft feedback can reduce mentor workload.\\nUser Stories Included\\nAI Rubric Evaluation\\nAI Draft Feedback Generation\"}]}, {\"rubric_id\": 35, \"status\": \"Yes\", \"chunks_ids_met\": [\"file_0_0\", \"file_0_2\", \"file_0_3\"], \"reason\": \"PRD fits one page, includes all 7 sections, and at least 3 open questions.\", \"confidence_score\": 1.0, \"evaluation_criteria\": \"Avoided: PRD fits one page with all 7 sections and at least 3 open questions.\", \"chunks_met\": [{\"id\": \"file_0_0\", \"content\": \"Product Requirements Document (PRD)\\n=========================================================================\\nProduct Requirements Document (PRD)\\nProduct Name\\nAI First-Pass Review Assistant + Rubric Auto-Checker\\nProblem Statement\\nThe current internship review process relies heavily on mentors and Engineering Managers to manually review submissions and provide feedback. As submission volume increases, review backlogs grow and Median Time-to-First-Feedback (TTFF) increases.\\nSprint 1 research revealed that mentors spend significant time repeating similar feedback across multiple submissions, while interns frequently lose momentum and engagement when feedback is delayed.\\nThis bottleneck limits the scalability of the Virtual Internship Program and creates an inconsistent learning experience. The opportunity is to reduce repetitive review effort through AI-assisted review workflows while maintaining human oversight and feedback quality.\\nPrimary User Persona\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_2\", \"content\": \"• Deliver faster feedback to interns.\\n• Increase consistency of review quality.\\nProduct Success Metrics:\\n• 30% reduction in TTFF.\\n• 20% reduction in mentor review time per submission.\\n• Mentor Override Rate below 40%.\\n• No reduction in feedback satisfaction scores.\\nAI Constraints\\nLatency:\\nAI review generation should complete within 10 seconds under normal operating conditions.\\nAccuracy:\\nAI-generated rubric assessments should achieve at least 80% agreement with mentor evaluations during testing.\\nCost Constraints:\\nThe solution must operate within approved AI infrastructure budgets. Average inference cost per review must remain sustainable at scale.\\nData Availability Constraints:\\nHistorical review data may be incomplete, inconsistent, or unavailable for certain submission categories. The MVP must function using structured rubrics even when historical examples are limited.\\nReliability Constraints:\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_3\", \"content\": \"Reliability Constraints:\\nThe system must provide fallback workflows when AI services fail, timeout, return low-confidence outputs, or encounter missing rubric data.\\n=========================================================================\\n7. Open Questions\\nWhat confidence score threshold should require mandatory mentor review?\\nWhich rubric formats are currently standardized across the platform?\\nHow should mentor edits be captured and used to improve future AI recommendations?\\nWhat level of explanation is necessary to build mentor trust in AI-generated feedback?\\nWhat fallback workflow should be triggered after repeated AI service failures?\\nWhich review categories are suitable for AI-assisted evaluation and which require fully manual review?\\nWhat success threshold will determine whether the MVP should move into full implementation?\\n=========================================================================\\nUser Stories & Acceptance Criteria\\nStory 1: AI Rubric Evaluation\"}]}, {\"rubric_id\": 36, \"status\": \"Avoided: 5-8 stories, each with at least 2 acceptance criteria in Given/When/Then format.\", \"chunks_met\": [{\"id\": \"file_0_4\", \"content\": \"User Stories & Acceptance Criteria\\nStory 1: AI Rubric Evaluation\\nAs a mentor, I want AI to evaluate submissions against rubric criteria so that I spend less time performing repetitive assessments.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven a valid submission and an associated rubric\\nWhen the mentor requests AI analysis\\nThen the system generates scores for each rubric criterion.\\nGiven rubric scores have been generated\\nWhen the mentor views the analysis\\nThen an explanation is displayed for each score.\\nStory 2: AI Draft Feedback Generation\\nAs a mentor, I want AI-generated draft feedback so that I can review submissions faster.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI analysis has completed successfully\\nWhen draft feedback is generated\\nThen the feedback is displayed in an editable format.\\nGiven AI-generated feedback is displayed\\nWhen the mentor edits the feedback\\nThen the updated version is saved before publishing.\\nStory 3: Mentor Approval Workflow\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_5\", \"content\": \"Then the updated version is saved before publishing.\\nStory 3: Mentor Approval Workflow\\nAs a mentor, I want to approve or reject AI-generated recommendations so that feedback quality remains under human control.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI recommendations are available\\nWhen the mentor selects Approve\\nThen the feedback is marked as approved and ready for publication.\\nGiven AI recommendations are available\\nWhen the mentor selects Reject\\nThen the AI recommendations are discarded and manual review remains available.\\nStory 4: Intern Feedback Delivery\\nAs an intern, I want to receive reviewed feedback quickly so that I can continue progressing through my internship tasks.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven mentor approval has been completed\\nWhen feedback is published\\nThen the intern receives access to the feedback.\\nGiven feedback is available\\nWhen the intern opens the review page\\nThen rubric scores and mentor feedback are displayed.\\nStory 5: AI Confidence Transparency\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_6\", \"content\": \"Then rubric scores and mentor feedback are displayed.\\nStory 5: AI Confidence Transparency\\nAs a mentor, I want confidence indicators for AI-generated reviews so that I can determine the appropriate level of oversight.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven AI analysis has completed\\nWhen confidence is calculated\\nThen a High, Medium, or Low confidence label is displayed.\\nGiven the confidence score is Low\\nWhen the mentor views the results\\nThen a warning message is shown recommending additional review.\\nStory 6: AI Timeout Handling (Edge Case)\\nAs a mentor, I want to continue reviewing submissions when AI services fail so that my work is never blocked.\\nAcceptance Criteria\\nGiven an AI request exceeds the allowed response time\\nWhen the request times out\\nThen the system displays a timeout message and retry option.\\nGiven an AI timeout occurs\\nWhen the mentor selects Continue Manually\\nThen the manual review workflow becomes available immediately.\\nStory 7: Missing Rubric Handling (Edge Case)\"}, {\"id\": \"file_0_7\", \"content\": \"Story 7: Missing Rubric Handling (Edge Case)\\nAs a #### 2026-08-03T07:01:39.494Z  ·  `trig_timer_brief`
*tags: task_kickoff, actor_response*
> Delivered the Sprint 4 initial task brief to Learner B6.
- **manager → learner:** Learner B6, you are assigned to complete the Sprint 4 brief. You are expected to deliver a complete product launch package and final demo presentation on schedule.
  
  Task Context
  Prepare the product for go-live by synthesizing your previous sprint work into an actionable go-to-market plan, experiment structure, dashboard, operational checklist, and final demo presentation.
  
  What You Must Build or Analyze
  - Go-To-Market (GTM) Plan: Define the target segment, core messaging, launch channels, rollout phases (beta to staged to full), and a detailed communication timeline.
  - Launch Checklist: Construct a checklist with at least 10 items spanning pre-launch, launch day, and post-launch. Each item must specify a role owner, status, and clear rollback/pause triggers.
  - A/B Experiment Design: Design an experiment for your riskiest assumption following the required hypothesis format ('If change X, then metric Y moves Z because W'), including control and variant definitions, minimum sample size, run duration, success metric linked to your Sprint 2 Metric Tree, and a pre-mortem analysis.
  - Metrics Dashboard Mockup: Track a minimum of 5 metrics from your Sprint 2 Metric Tree, defining for each metric its definition, tracking cadence, owner, and action triggers.
  - Final Demo Day Deck: Create a presentation deck following the full 6-part narrative arc: problem, research, decision, solution, metrics, and next steps.
  
  Required Deliverables
  Submit your strategy and launch documentation (GTM Plan, Launch Checklist, A/B Experiment Design, Metrics Dashboard Mockup) alongside your Final Demo Day Presentation Deck.
  
  Assets and Links
  No external assets or links are provided with this task. Work directly from your established Sprint 1 research, Sprint 2 strategy, and Sprint 3 prototype artifacts.
  
  Deadline
  Thursday, August 6, 2026, at 11:59 PM Cairo time. The deadline stands and extensions will not be granted.
  
  Success Standard
  All 5 deliverables must be complete, fully aligned with your earlier sprint outputs, and demonstrative of a complete product journey.
  
  Support Routing
  Submit your completed work and direct all technical questions or draft feedback to your mentor. For any non-technical, operational, administrative, or platform access issues, post in the internship group and mention the internship group admins. Proceed with execution immediately.
